<a id="resources"></a>
## 📖 Appendix C — Further Resources

### OWASP "Top 10" Reference Suite (read all of these)

These are the canonical, regularly-updated references that every security professional cites. Free, vendor-neutral, community-maintained.

- **OWASP Top 10 (Web Application)** — `https://owasp.org/Top10/` — current edition is **2025** (it supersedes 2021; SSRF folded into A01, supply chain promoted to A03, new A10 for mishandled exceptions). The starting point for every web security conversation.
- **OWASP API Security Top 10 (2023)** — `https://owasp.org/API-Security/` — separates API-specific risks (BOLA, BFLA, unrestricted resource consumption) from generic web risks.
- **OWASP Top 10 for LLM Applications** — `https://genai.owasp.org/llm-top-10/` — the AI/LLM equivalent, now maintained under the OWASP GenAI Security Project (the old `owasp.org/www-project-top-10-for-large-language-model-applications/` page keeps only historical releases). The current edition is **2026**, published August 2026 — get it from `https://genai.owasp.org/resource/owasp-genai-llm-top-10-2026/`. **Watch out:** as of this writing the project's own `/llm-top-10/` landing page still lists the ten risk cards under the old `LLMxx:2025` numbering, so the first page you land on may not be the current list. The table above uses the 2026 numbering. Covered in Module 25.7.
- **OWASP Mobile Top 10 (2024)** — `https://owasp.org/www-project-mobile-top-10/` — covered in Module 25.5.
- **OWASP CI/CD Top 10** — `https://owasp.org/www-project-top-10-ci-cd-security-risks/` — supply-chain risks in your build pipeline.
- **OWASP Cheat Sheet Series** — `https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/` — concise, language-specific implementation guides for every Top 10 entry.

### Free Practice Platforms
- **TryHackMe** — tryhackme.com — guided rooms, gamified, free tier excellent
- **HackTheBox** — hackthebox.com — harder; "Starting Point" track is free
- **OverTheWire** — overthewire.org — classic wargames, pure terminal
- **PicoCTF** — picoctf.org — Carnegie Mellon's beginner CTF, runs year-round
- **PortSwigger Web Security Academy** — portswigger.net/web-security — FREE, best web security training anywhere
- **RangeForce Community Edition** — free blue team labs
- **LetsDefend** — letsdefend.io — blue team scenarios
- **Damn Vulnerable GraphQL Application (DVGA)** — `https://github.com/dolevf/Damn-Vulnerable-GraphQL-Application` — specifically for GraphQL practice
- **OWASP Juice Shop** — `https://github.com/juice-shop/juice-shop` — best-in-class web pentest playground (native ARM64 Docker image)

### Free / Cheap Courses
- **Cybrary** — free courses, many certification-aligned
- **SANS Cyber Aces** — free foundational courses from SANS
- **Professor Messer** — YouTube, full Security+ course free
- **John Hammond** — YouTube, CTF walkthroughs
- **LiveOverflow** — YouTube, binary exploitation and CTFs

### Books
- *The Web Application Hacker's Handbook* — Stuttard & Pinto
- *The Tangled Web* — Zalewski (browser security)
- *Practical Malware Analysis* — Sikorski & Honig
- *Hacking: The Art of Exploitation* — Erickson
- *Blue Team Handbook* — Murdoch
- *The Hacker Playbook 3* — Kim (offensive)

### Communities
- r/netsec, r/AskNetsec (Reddit)
- Discord: TryHackMe, HackTheBox, The Cyber Mentor
- Twitter/X infosec community (follow researchers, not influencers)
- Local DEF CON groups (DC###) and BSides conferences (often free/cheap for students)

### News (daily, 10 min)
- The Hacker News — thehackernews.com
- BleepingComputer
- Krebs on Security
- Risky Business podcast (weekly)

---

## 🧭 Final Words

You picked a field where the only constant is change. Tools will come and go; the foundations you built here — how networks work, how trust is established, how humans are fooled, how systems fail, how defenders think — these outlast any specific tool.

Three commitments to yourself going forward:

1. **Learn in public.** A blog, a GitHub, a Twitter presence. Your portfolio opens doors your resume can't.
2. **Stay ethical, always.** The community has a long memory. A single illegal shortcut follows you for a decade.
3. **Teach the next one.** You learned for free from a community. Pay it forward. Explain concepts to a beginner at least once a month. It also cements your own understanding faster than anything else.

Welcome to the field, Cyber Guardian. We're glad to have you.

> *"Amateurs hack systems. Professionals hack people. The best among us protect both."*
